Figure
23
shows the dependence of

\mathrm{d}n/\mathrm{d}\eta
and
\langle p_{\mathrm{T}}\rangle
measured at mid-rapidity on the number of
participating nucleons in the collisions,
\langle N_{\mathrm{part}}\rangle
.
For
\langle N_{\mathrm{part}}\rangle
values below approximately 25, corresponding to the 10%
and more peripheral centrality intervals, the measured
(1/\langle N_{\mathrm{part}}\rangle)\;\!\mathrm{d}n/\mathrm{d}\eta

in
O
+
O
and
Ne
+
Ne
are consistent, within uncertainties.
In this region, the
\mathrm{d}n/\mathrm{d}\eta
dependence is approximately linear in

\langle N_{\mathrm{part}}\rangle
.
At larger
\langle N_{\mathrm{part}}\rangle
, the
\mathrm{d}n/\mathrm{d}\eta
and
\langle p_{\mathrm{T}}\rangle
values
rise more sharply as the maximum
N_{\text{part}}
values are approached.
This rise is consistent with the behavior observed in ultracentral collisions in

Pb
+
Pb
and
Xe
+
Xe
collisions
[
16
,
83
]
, where for small impact parameters the
transverse area of the medium becomes constant, but initial-state
fluctuations increase the energy density leading to enhanced radial
flow and, thus, higher
\langle p_{\mathrm{T}}\rangle
.
Figure
24
compares the

\langle N_{\mathrm{part}}\rangle
dependence of the
\mathrm{d}n/\mathrm{d}\eta
and
\langle p_{\mathrm{T}}\rangle
measurements at
mid-rapidity with model calculations.
The trends observed here are similar to those in
Figures
21
and
22
:
for
\mathrm{d}n/\mathrm{d}\eta
, the models are closer to the data in central collisions
but underestimate it in peripheral collisions, while for
\langle p_{\mathrm{T}}\rangle
,
the data lie between the different model predictions.

The centrality dependence of the ratio
Ne
+

/

+

for (a)

and (b)

, measured at mid-pseudorapidity (

).
Similar results are also shown for (c)

,

).

[

]
(labeled “IPGlasma”)
and Ref.

]

The model calculations in all cases are for the

case,
and are identical in the upper and lower panels.

Figure
25
shows the
Ne
+
Ne
/
O
+
O
ratios
for
\mathrm{d}n/\mathrm{d}\eta
,
\mathrm{d}n/\mathrm{d}y_{\pi}
, and
\langle p_{\mathrm{T}}\rangle
measured at mid-rapidity,
for results extrapolated to
p_{\mathrm{T}}>0\penalty\ \text{MeV}
.
Figures
65
–
68

in the Appendix show similar comparisons
for the
p_{\text{T}}>0.27

GeV
fiducial region, together with the
measured
\mathrm{d}n/\mathrm{d}\eta
,
\mathrm{d}n/\mathrm{d}y_{\pi}
, and
\langle p_{\mathrm{T}}\rangle
values, as a function of centrality.
For
\mathrm{d}n/\mathrm{d}\eta
, the ratio is approximately 1.2 in 0–1% centrality
collisions, increases slightly over the 0–5% centrality range, and
then decreases monotonically to approximately 1.04 by the 75–80%
centrality interval.
Comparisons with model calculations are also shown in Figure
25
.
In the IPGlasma- and Trento-based hydrodynamic model calculations,
the increase in the ratio over the 0–5% centrality
collisions is significantly larger, particularly for the IPGlasma
model, and persists over the 0–20% centrality range.
The subsequent decrease toward peripheral collisions is substantially
weaker than that observed in the data.
The EPOS model calculations are consistent with the data throughout
the measured multiplicity range.
For the
\langle p_{\mathrm{T}}\rangle
ratios, the data exhibit variations
typically less than
\pm 1\%
across the measured centrality range.
The measured ratios lie between the different model predictions.
The results for
|y_{\pi}|<0.5
are nearly identical to the
measurements for
|\eta|<0.5
.
For these ratios, the measurements for the
p_{\text{T}}>0.27

GeV
fiducial region are also
nearly identical to the
p_{\mathrm{T}}>0\penalty\ \text{MeV}
extrapolation case as shown
in Figures
65
–
68
.
The uncertainty in these measured ratios is smaller than the differences
between predictions obtained with different nuclear-structure models
(PGCM and NLEFT), indicating that these measurements are sensitive to
the modeling of the nuclear structure.

7
Conclusion

This paper presents measurements of charged-particle
p_{\text{T}}
spectra,
multiplicity, and mean transverse momentum in
O
+
O
and
Ne
+
Ne
collisions
at 5.36
TeV
per nucleon pair, using the ATLAS detector at the LHC.
The integrated luminosities of the
O
+
O
and
Ne
+
Ne
data sets
used in this measurement are
27.7\penalty\ \mu\text{b}^{-1}
and

53.1\penalty\ \mu\text{b}^{-1}
, respectively.
The measurements are performed using charged-particle tracks
reconstructed in the inner detector over the fiducial phase space

0.27<p_{\text{T}}<5
GeV and
|\eta|<2.5
.
Results are reported as a function of pseudorapidity and collision
centrality, where the latter is characterized by the total
transverse energy measured in the forward calorimeters.
Reconstruction efficiencies and fake- and secondary-track contributions
are corrected using Monte Carlo simulations, and a comprehensive
evaluation of systematic uncertainties is performed.
Systematic uncertainties in the measurements arise from the track
selection, detector material description, centrality determination,
and procedure for extrapolation to
p_{\mathrm{T}}>0\penalty\ \text{MeV}
.

Measurements are also performed as a function of rapidity
y_{\pi}
computed
for the charged-pion mass hypothesis.
The use of
y_{\pi}
significantly reduces effects arising from the
difference between pseudorapidity and true rapidity, particularly
near mid-rapidity, providing results closer to those that would be
obtained using true rapidity.
The dynamics of particle production are expected to be weakly
dependent on rapidity, a feature reflected in the measurements
presented as a function of
y_{\pi}
.

Ratios of the
p_{\text{T}}
spectra between central and peripheral collisions are measured.
These indicate a hardening of the
p_{\text{T}}
spectra from peripheral to central
collisions for
p_{\text{T}}\lesssim 2

GeV
, and demonstrate the buildup of radial flow.
This buildup is found to be similar at mid-rapidity and forward rapidity,
consistent with the approximate boost invariance of radial flow.
Measurements of the
Ne
+
Ne
/
O
+
O
ratio of the spectra for a given centrality
are found to be nearly independent of
p_{\text{T}}
.

The charged-particle multiplicity and mean transverse momentum are
both measured within the fiducial acceptance and extrapolated to
p_{\mathrm{T}}>0\penalty\ \text{MeV}
,
using fits to the measured
p_{\text{T}}
spectra.
The results show a smooth evolution of
\mathrm{d}n/\mathrm{d}\eta
and
\langle p_{\mathrm{T}}\rangle
with
centrality and (pseudo-)rapidity in both collision systems.
For a given centrality interval,
Ne
+
Ne
collisions exhibit larger
charged-particle multiplicities than
O
+
O
collisions.
The
Ne
+
Ne
/
O
+
O
ratio of
\mathrm{d}n/\mathrm{d}\eta
values varies between approximately
1.05 and 1.20, while the corresponding ratio of
\langle p_{\mathrm{T}}\rangle
values varies
between approximately 0.99 and 1.01.
These ratios are consistent between the
\eta
- and
y_{\pi}
-based measurements.
The
Ne
+
Ne
/
O
+
O
ratios are also found to be consistent between the fiducial
and
p_{\mathrm{T}}>0\penalty\ \text{MeV}
measurements.
The multiplicities and
\langle p_{\mathrm{T}}\rangle
exhibit a significantly weaker dependence
as a function of
y_{\pi}
than as a function of
\eta
, reflecting
the approximate boost invariance of particle production.

The measurements are compared with hydrodynamic model calculations
in which the initial energy densities are computed using the
IPGlasma and Trento frameworks.
These frameworks incorporate state-of-the-art nuclear-structure
models for oxygen and neon to provide initial conditions for
simulations of the quark–gluon plasma evolution.
The IPGlasma calculations are coupled to the MUSIC hydrodynamic
package, while the Trento calculations use the Trajectum package.
Comparisons with the EPOS event generator are also made.
The model calculations generally reproduce the observed trends,
but exhibit some deviations from the data
that increase for peripheral collisions.
Among the models considered, EPOS provides the best overall
agreement with the data, while IPGlasma-based predictions
show slightly better agreement than those based on Trento.
Comparisons of the
Ne
+
Ne
/
O
+
O
ratios for the
p_{\text{T}}
spectra,
\mathrm{d}n/\mathrm{d}\eta
and
\langle p_{\mathrm{T}}\rangle
with
Trento calculations using the PGCM and NLEFT nuclear-structure models
demonstrate that the measurements presented here provide sensitivity to
differences between nuclear-structure models.

These measurements constitute the first detailed ATLAS results for
charged-particle production in
O
+
O
and
Ne
+
Ne
collisions at the
LHC.
They provide important experimental constraints on models of particle
production and collective behavior in small- and intermediate-sized
nuclear systems, and complement existing measurements in
pp
,
p
+
A
,
and heavy-ion collisions.
Together, the
\mathrm{d}n/\mathrm{d}\eta
and
\langle p_{\mathrm{T}}\rangle
measurements reported here provide
a baseline for understanding particle production and collective
effects in light-ion collisions, and contribute to a more complete
picture of the onset and evolution of quark–gluon plasma dynamics
as a function of system size.

Appendix

Figure
26
shows the ratios of the
p_{\text{T}}
spectra measured
in
|\eta|<0.5
to those measured in
1.5<|\eta|<2.25
,
for
O
+
O
and
Ne
+
Ne
collisions.
Figure
27
shows the corresponding ratios for spectra measured
in intervals of
y_{\pi}
, comparing
|y_{\pi}|<0.5
with
1.5<|y_{\pi}|<2.25
.
In Figure
26
, a depletion is observed at
p_{\text{T}}\lesssim 0.6\penalty\ \text{GeV}
,
which is absent in Figure
27
.
This depletion is an artifact of presenting the results in
intervals of
\eta
.
It arises from the
\eta
-migration discussed in Section
4.2
,
which is enhanced at low
p_{\text{T}}
and near
\eta\sim 0
, and modifies the shape of the
p_{\text{T}}
spectra.

The remaining figures and tables in the Appendix support results shown in the
main body of the paper and are referenced there;
they are therefore not discussed further here.

|y_{\pi}|<0.5
,
(instead of
|\eta|<0.5
).
However, the theory calculations are for
|\eta|<0.5
, and in the case of the
Trento model, always for the
p_{\mathrm{T}}>0\penalty\ \text{MeV}
case.
Panel (d) is identical to Figure
25
d.
